Remarks.&mdash;During April the highest reading of the barometer was 30.316, on the 4th; and the lowest 29.872, on the 20th. The highest temperature was 90&quot;, on the 27th; and the lowest 540, on the 2nd. S.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the nth, averaging 13.2 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 16 days, measuring 7.72 inches. During May the highest reading of the barometer was 30.260, on the 3rd; and the lowest 29.500, on the 29th. The highest temperature was 930, on the 30th; and the lowest 64&deg;, on the 2nd and 3rd. S.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 2nd, averaging 11.6 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 22 days, measuring 10.81 inches. During June the highest reading of the barometer was 30.050, on the 3rd; and the lowest 29.750, on the 15th. The highest temperature was 96&deg;, on the 12th and 26th; and the lowest 70&deg;, on the 6th and 7th. S.W. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 30th, averaging 16.6 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 18 days, measuring 13.29 inches. IX. * * 166 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, 1892.] NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. &#39; No. 254. CHEFOO DISTRICT. * &#39; i. * &#39; . j NORTH-EAST SHANTUNG PROMONTORY LIGHTHOUSE. N Notice is hereby given of the intended alteration of the present light at this station, which is a fixed one, into an occulting light. Also that it is intended to establish a fog siren at this station, which will sound a high and a low note. Shanghai, i 2th April 1892. No. 255. YANGTZE RIVER&mdash;CHINKIANG DISTRICT. NORTH TREE LIGHT. Notice is hereby given that the North Tree Light has been shifted 770 feet N. 50&deg; E. from its last position, in consequence of the washing away of the river bank. Shanghai, 2Sth May 1892.

1; l-fl&#39;l&#39; &#39; f&quot;&#39; &sect; &#39; &laquo; : APPENDIX II. KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. APRIL-JUNE 1892. 25 i
194 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, (Received 12th July 1892J CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, 8th July 1892. SIR, I.&mdash;a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the second quarter of the year 1892, April-June. b. The Custom House (ft M 11) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.Tk 30 and Hk.Tk 80 per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the 1st July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon ($&pound; P&reg;J), Changchow (J| ^[&amp;gt;|); Fotochow (ft i&laquo;)&amp;gt; and Kowloon g|) stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II. MOVEMENTS OF VESSELS.&mdash;The number and movements of vessels, inwards and outwards, during the quarter, are set forth in the following Tables :&mdash; i&deg;. CAPSUIMOON. JUNKS. April. May. June. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the second quarter of the year 1892, April-June. h. The Custom House ( 11) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.Tts. 30 and Hk.Tts. 80 per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com&not; menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the 1st July 1887. APPENDIX III. LUNGCHOW AND MENGTSZ: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. APRIL-JUNE 1892. 4 &bull;
214 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, (Received 28th July 1892.) CUSTOM HOUSE, LUNGCHOW, 5th July 1892. SIR, I.&mdash;111 conformity with the instructions contained in your Circulars Nos. 21 of 1869, 14 of 1872, and 6 of 1875, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Lungchow for the second quarter of the year 1892. II. REPORTS TO THE CUSTOMS (i.e., Arrivals and Departures). April. May. June. Total. 1892.] MENGTSZ. 217 (Received 13th August 1892.) CUSTOM HOUSE, MENGTSZ, 15th July 1892. SIR, I.&mdash;a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circulars Nos. 21 of 1869, 14 of 1872, and 6 of 1875, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Mengtsz, Manhao, and Mapai-kuan for the second quarter of the year 1892. b. The head office (|&sect; d}&sect;) and the branch office at Manhao (|&sect;| were opened on the 24th August 1889; the branch office at Mapai-kuan (H |&sect;f]) on the 19th September, three weeks later.
